### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the staged repository/data inputs relevant to the capital PCA (especially capital.csv and any analysis scripts or notebooks). Report the available files/schema and basic data dimensions or missingness, outline a suitable workflow to characterize House Size, Daggers, and Hatchets/Swords on PC1 and their distribution on PC2 in 100-year slices, and flag consequential assumptions or uncertainties (including PCA variable scaling, slice construction, and component sign/orientation). Do not yet execute the full analysis or report inferred findings.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The brief specifies a data-grounded hypothesis about the symbolic-capital variables' PC1 values and PC2 distribution in hundred-year bins. The data dictionary identifies the relevant capital.csv fields, but gives no PCA procedure, binning convention, or results.

**Open objectives:** Inspect staged inputs and analysis materials; Propose an analysis approach for symbolic-capital PCA across 100-year slices; Identify consequential methodological assumptions and uncertainties

**Agent reply (plan)**

I inspected the staged files and metadata only; I have not run the PCA or inferred results. The workspace contains `data/capital.csv`, `data/time_series_data.csv`, `data/pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv`, and `task.json`; no analysis scripts or notebooks were staged.

**Relevant input:** `capital.csv` has 39 rows × 15 columns: BCE, group/color labels, and 12 capital indicators. BCE ranges from 4300 to 500 in 100-year steps, with 39 unique values and no duplicate years. Pandas initially reads the indicator fields as strings, not numbers, because values use decimal commas (e.g. `-0,44`). The CSV reports no nulls, but this does not rule out malformed/non-numeric entries. The supplied data dictionary identifies `ZAxtSchwert` (hatchets/swords), `ZDolch` (daggers), and `Zhausgr` (house size); the other nine indicators are copper/gold, amber, monument count, depot, sickle, axes/celts, monument size, pottery form, and pottery decoration. `group` and `color` are described as dropped after loading. The two other staged datasets are time-series/pollen inputs and do not appear necessary for this specific forms-of-capital PCA question.

**Proposed workflow after approval:** parse decimal-comma indicator strings as numeric; verify parsing, missingness, ranges, and year order; retain the capital variables and exclude BCE/group/color from the PCA. Confirm the analysis set and whether rows represent successive century observations. For each 100-year time slice, perform PCA across all capital indicators, recording variable loadings (and optionally scores) for PC1 and PC2, variance explained, and signs. Summarize the three named symbolic indicators' PC1 loading/value and their PC2 distribution across slices, preserving the time labels and reporting any changing patterns as descriptive rather than causal. A compact table and/or plot can make slice-wise variation legible.

**Consequential assumptions / uncertainties:** (1) All indicators are labeled Z-values, suggesting comparable standardized scales, but it is unclear whether to use them as-is (covariance PCA) or re-standardize each slice (correlation PCA); this may change loadings. (2) “Hundred-year time slices” could refer to the rows already sampled every 100 years, or to bins applied to finer-grained observations; here the file appears to contain one row per century. Need be clear about BCE boundary conventions and ascending/descending display order. (3) PCA component signs are arbitrary and can flip independently by slice, so PC1/PC2 orientation must be aligned or interpreted relative to a documented anchor; raw signs alone are not intrinsically meaningful. (4) It is not explicit whether “values on PC1” means variable loadings, scores of observations, or both; symbolic capital components as variables most naturally suggests loadings. (5) The intended PCA may derive from an external/scripted method not included in the staged repository; row-wise PCA separately within each century is impossible with one observation per slice, so a PCA for each time slice likely requires a window/grouping or is actually one PCA followed by slice-wise score/loading summaries. This structural ambiguity is material and should be resolved from study context or a supplied analysis recipe before a full analysis.

**Shared evidence:**

Repository listing showed only task.json and three CSV files, no .py/.ipynb/.R analyses. `capital.csv` read as (39,15), BCE=4300..500 with 39 unique values, no duplicate BCE and zero parsed nulls; capital-indicator columns had string dtype and comma decimals. Metadata names the target fields and describes group/color as dropped post-load. No PCA or substantive result has been computed.

**Assumptions:** The metadata schema is accurate for the staged CSV.; capital.csv is the intended source for forms-of-capital indicators.

**Uncertainties:** The phrase 'PCA ... in hundred-year time slices' does not specify PCA fitting population/window versus slice-wise summarization.; Whether Z fields should be used directly or standardized per slice is unspecified.; PC1/PC2 might mean variable loadings or observation scores; sign orientation convention is unspecified.

### 2. Researcher — refine_analysis

Thanks—the inspection is useful. Before the full analysis, please resolve the structural ambiguity by inspecting any available study recipe/context beyond the CSV metadata and determine what “PCA in 100-year time slices” can operationally mean for these 39 rows. In particular, with one observation per century, do not fit a separate PCA to each single row. Recommend a defensible analysis consistent with the question: for example, one PCA across observations and indicators, then report the three variables’ loadings on PC1/PC2 alongside century-specific observation scores; or use a clearly justified multi-century window if there is evidence that is the intended procedure. State whether the requested PC1 values are interpreted as loadings, scores, or both, and specify a transparent scale/sign convention. You may then proceed with the analysis under that stated approach, noting the ambiguity rather than treating any assumption as confirmed.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The agent reports 39 unique annual-century observations, one row per 100-year step, so per-slice PCA on individual rows is not viable. PCA population/window and whether “values” refers to loadings or scores remain unresolved; I am requesting a focused operational recommendation before authorizing the main analysis.

**Open objectives:** Resolve operational interpretation of PCA by 100-year slices for one-row-per-century data; Specify target PCA quantities and scaling/sign conventions; After clarification, analyze and submit a data-grounded hypothesis

**Agent reply (plan)**

I checked all available study context beyond the CSV: `task.json` and its embedded `datasets.json` dictionary are the only contextual/recipe materials, and the workspace contains no PCA script, notebook, or other study documentation. Nothing staged specifies a moving window, per-century PCA, scaling recipe, or component orientation. The 39 rows are annual-bin observations at BCE 4300, 4200, …, 500, so a separate PCA per century cannot be estimated from one row per slice.

**Recommended operational interpretation:** fit one PCA across all 39 century observations, using the 12 capital indicators as variables (exclude BCE/group/color); report the three target variables' *loadings* on PC1 and PC2 as their stable variable characterization, and separately report each century's *observation scores* on PC1/PC2 to show their time distribution. This is more defensible than inventing a multi-century window without documentary evidence. The repeated rows are not independent temporal replicates, so frame PCA descriptively. This interpretation distinguishes loadings (how each capital indicator aligns with an axis) from scores (where each century falls on the axes); “PC1 values” is best read as loadings given the question names the symbolic capital components, but both are useful to include.

**Scale/sign proposal:** parse decimal commas; use the supplied Z-valued indicators as-is, centered by PCA, with no additional per-variable scaling (thus a covariance PCA on already standardized indicators; verify that all columns are indeed intended Z values). For a reproducible orientation, after fitting orient each component so the loading of the highest-absolute-loading variable on that component is positive; list the anchor per PC. Since the question appears to concern their relative pattern, report magnitudes and relative signs as well as oriented signs. Signs remain conventions, not substantive absolute meanings.

For the century “slices,” treat each row as a labeled 100-year time point/interval as encoded by its BCE label, not a re-averaged wider interval; display scores by BCE in time order. Acknowledge the unresolved convention for interpreting whether a label marks the start, end, or midpoint of the century, since the data only labels rows by BCE. No evidence supports a sliding/multi-century PCA window. I can now proceed with this explicitly stated descriptive workflow unless you prefer otherwise; the required plan-stage approval remains outstanding, so I will wait before executing the main PCA.

**Shared evidence:**

`find /workspace` yielded only task.json and the three CSV inputs. Grep found only task text/dataset descriptions, no analysis recipe. The capital table previously inspected has 39 unique BCE values from 4300 down to 500 at 100-year intervals. The task metadata calls each capital column a Z value but does not define PCA fitting or orientation.

**Assumptions:** The requested task can be answered with a global PCA plus century-specific scores rather than an undocumented sliding-window procedure.; The capital fields are standardized Z indicators and are appropriate for PCA without another scaling pass.; The row's BCE value can be treated as a century label, while its precise within-century boundary meaning is unknown.

**Uncertainties:** No method recipe confirms global PCA over 39 rows or rules out an external moving-window method.; Variable loadings versus observation scores are not disambiguated in the question; plan is to report both.; No source defines whether BCE labels mark interval starts, ends, or midpoints.

### 3. Researcher — next_task

The proposed global PCA over all 39 century observations, followed by reporting target-variable loadings and century-specific scores, is a defensible response to the one-row-per-century structure and absence of a documented window method. Proceed with that explicitly provisional interpretation: use the 12 capital indicators, parse decimal commas, retain the provided Z scales without extra scaling, document the component sign convention and the BCE-label ambiguity, and report findings as descriptive. Include PC1/PC2 loadings for House Size, Daggers, and Hatchets/Swords, plus scores or a concise account of their distribution across the century observations. Do not claim this recovers a confirmed original study recipe.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** The handoff reports 39 unique observations spaced 100 years apart, with one row per century; available context contains no PCA recipe or script. It proposes a single global PCA and distinguishes variable loadings from century scores while noting unresolved scaling/sign and interval-label conventions.

**Open objectives:** Execute and report the data-grounded PCA characterization of House Size, Daggers, and Hatchets/Swords on PC1 and across PC2 by century, under the explicitly provisional global-PCA interpretation.

**Agent reply (result)**

**Provisional, descriptive result (not a confirmed reconstruction of an original study recipe):** Because the file has one row at each of 39 century labels and no documented multi-century window method, I fit one PCA to all 39 rows using the 12 capital indicators and then examined each century’s PC scores. The three symbolic-capital variables have positive loadings on both axes under the stated sign convention. In particular, House Size (`Zhausgr`) has PC1 loading **0.228** and PC2 **0.364**; Daggers (`ZDolch`) **0.279** and **0.120**; Hatchets/Swords (`ZAxtSchwert`) **0.301** and **0.067**. Thus the three align positively with PC1, while House Size has the clearest relative association with PC2 among them; Daggers and especially Hatchets/Swords are more concentrated on PC1 than PC2. PC1 explains 31.8% of variance, PC2 20.4% (52.3% combined).

The century-specific scores show a broad temporal separation on PC1: BCE 4300–4100 are all about **−1.71**, most observations from 4000–2200 BCE are negative, scores rise around 2100–1800 BCE, become strongly positive from 1600 BCE onward, peak at **4.45 at 1100 BCE**, then remain positive through 500 BCE. PC2 is more variable/non-monotonic: it is strongly negative at 4300–4100 (**−3.79**), mostly positive across 4000–2200 (with maximum **2.41 at 3200 BCE**), mixed around 2100–1100, and largely negative from 1000 through 500 BCE. These are scores of the whole 12-indicator profiles, not values specific to a single symbolic indicator.

**Method and convention:** Comma-decimal fields were parsed as numbers; all 12 supplied Z-indicators were used as-is with mean-centering and no extra scaling (covariance PCA). BCE, `group`, and `color` were excluded. Component orientation is made reproducible by forcing the largest-absolute-loading variable to be positive on each component: `ZCU_AU` anchors PC1 and `ZBeil` anchors PC2. PCA signs are arbitrary; positive/negative here only have meaning under this convention. The BCE entries are treated as century labels; the dataset does not say whether they denote interval starts, ends, or midpoints.

**Interpretation limits:** The “PC1 values” are reported as variable loadings, and the across-century distribution is reported as row-level observation scores. The staged materials do not establish that the original analysis used this global fitting scheme, direct Z scale, or this sign convention; nor do they document an alternative window or slice recipe. Accordingly, this answers the question with a defensible descriptive analysis, not a claim about a confirmed original recipe.

**Shared evidence:**

Executed `/workspace/capital_pca.py` successfully using NumPy eigendecomposition (scikit-learn is unavailable). It validated 39×12 finite numeric input, zero missing values, and 39 BCE labels spaced by 100 years from 4300 to 500. PC1/PC2 variance ratios were 0.3184/0.2042. Target loadings: Zhausgr (0.2283, 0.3644), ZDolch (0.2793, 0.1195), ZAxtSchwert (0.3010, 0.0666). PC1 scores range −2.0135 to 4.4519; PC2 −3.7878 to 2.4137. Script prints all 39 score pairs in descending BCE order and can be rerun from the workspace.

**Assumptions:** One global PCA across 39 century observations is used as the only supported, defensible interpretation given one observation per century and no evidence for a window procedure.; The 12 capital-indicator variables are already in meaningful Z scales, so no further standardization is applied.; BCE values label the century observations, without specifying interval boundary convention.

**Uncertainties:** The staged materials do not confirm the original study's PCA fitting procedure, scaling, or slice definition.; PCA component signs are arbitrary; the reported directions depend on the documented anchor convention.; The phrase PC1 values could mean loadings or observation scores; both were provided distinctly.

### 4. Researcher — conclude

Under the stated provisional, descriptive global-PCA approach, House Size, Daggers, and Hatchets/Swords all load positively on PC1 (0.228, 0.279, and 0.301). House Size has a larger PC2 loading (0.364) than Daggers (0.120) or Hatchets/Swords (0.067), indicating a comparatively stronger association with PC2; the latter two are more concentrated on PC1. Century observation scores show a broad shift from negative to positive PC1 values over time, while PC2 is less monotonic. The fitting scheme, direct use of Z scales, and sign orientation are explicit assumptions rather than confirmed details of the original analysis.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The reported executed analysis used 39 century observations and 12 indicators, with no missing values, and gives target-variable loadings and variance explained (PC1 31.8%, PC2 20.4%). It distinguishes variable loadings from century scores and documents the sign convention and method limitations. These results address the requested characterization while preserving the unresolved uncertainty about the original PCA recipe.

**Open objectives:** None

The workspace contains `data/capital.csv`, `data/time_series_data.csv`, `data/pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv`, and `task.json`; no analysis scripts or notebooks were staged.\\n\\n**Relevant input:** `capital.csv` has 39 rows \\u00d7 15 columns: BCE, group/color labels, and 12 capital indicators. BCE ranges from 4300 to 500 in 100-year steps, with 39 unique values and no duplicate years. Pandas initially reads the indicator fields as strings, not numbers, because values use decimal commas (e.g. `-0,44`). The CSV reports no nulls, but this does not rule out malformed/non-numeric entries. The supplied data dictionary identifies `ZAxtSchwert` (hatchets/swords), `ZDolch` (daggers), and `Zhausgr` (house size); the other nine indicators are copper/gold, amber, monument count, depot, sickle, axes/celts, monument size, pottery form, and pottery decoration. `group` and `color` are described as dropped after loading. The two other staged datasets are time-series/pollen inputs and do not appear necessary for this specific forms-of-capital PCA question.\\n\\n**Proposed workflow after approval:** parse decimal-comma indicator strings as numeric; verify parsing, missingness, ranges, and year order; retain the capital variables and exclude BCE/group/color from the PCA. Confirm the analysis set and whether rows represent successive century observations. For each 100-year time slice, perform PCA across all capital indicators, recording variable loadings (and optionally scores) for PC1 and PC2, variance explained, and signs. Summarize the three named symbolic indicators' PC1 loading/value and their PC2 distribution across slices, preserving the time labels and reporting any changing patterns as descriptive rather than causal. A compact table and/or plot can make slice-wise variation legible.\\n\\n**Consequential assumptions / uncertainties:** (1) All indicators are labeled Z-values, suggesting comparable standardized scales, but it is unclear whether to use them as-is (covariance PCA) or re-standardize each slice (correlation PCA); this may change loadings. (2) \\u201cHundred-year time slices\\u201d could refer to the rows already sampled every 100 years, or to bins applied to finer-grained observations; here the file appears to contain one row per century. Need be clear about BCE boundary conventions and ascending/descending display order. (3) PCA component signs are arbitrary and can flip independently by slice, so PC1/PC2 orientation must be aligned or interpreted relative to a documented anchor; raw signs alone are not intrinsically meaningful. (4) It is not explicit whether \\u201cvalues on PC1\\u201d means variable loadings, scores of observations, or both; symbolic capital components as variables most naturally suggests loadings. (5) The intended PCA may derive from an external/scripted method not included in the staged repository; row-wise PCA separately within each century is impossible with one observation per slice, so a PCA for each time slice likely requires a window/grouping or is actually one PCA followed by slice-wise score/loading summaries. This structural ambiguity is material and should be resolved from study context or a supplied analysis recipe before a full analysis.\", \"evidence\": \"Repository listing showed only task.json and three CSV files, no .py/.ipynb/.R analyses. `capital.csv` read as (39,15), BCE=4300..500 with 39 unique values, no duplicate BCE and zero parsed nulls; capital-indicator columns had string dtype and comma decimals. Metadata names the target fields and describes group/color as dropped post-load. No PCA or substantive result has been computed.\", \"assumptions\": [\"The metadata schema is accurate for the staged CSV.\", \"capital.csv is the intended source for forms-of-capital indicators.\"], \"uncertainties\": [\"The phrase 'PCA ... in hundred-year time slices' does not specify PCA fitting population/window versus slice-wise summarization.\", \"Whether Z fields should be used directly or standardized per slice is unspecified.\", \"PC1/PC2 might mean variable loadings or observation scores; sign orientation convention is unspecified.\"]}",

{
  "stage": "plan",
  "answer": "I checked all available study context beyond the CSV: `task.json` and its embedded `datasets.json` dictionary are the only contextual/recipe materials, and the workspace contains no PCA script, notebook, or other study documentation. Nothing staged specifies a moving window, per-century PCA, scaling recipe, or component orientation. {
  "command": "cat > /workspace/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\n\npath='data/capital.csv'\ndf=pd.read_csv(path, dtype=str)\nfeatures=[c for c in df.columns if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\n# CSV encodes decimals with commas. Parse all 12 indicator fields explicitly.\nX=df[features].apply(lambda col: pd.to_numeric(col.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='raise'))\nbce=pd.to_numeric(df['BCE'], errors='raise').astype(int)\nassert X.shape == (39,12) and X.notna().all().all()\nassert sorted(bce.unique()) == list(range(500,4301,100))\n# Use supplied Z-scale as-is; sklearn PCA centers but does not rescale variables.\npca=PCA(n_components=2)\nscores=pca.fit_transform(X)\nloadings=pca.components_.T\n# Deterministic sign orientation: largest absolute loading is positive for each axis.\nanchors=[]\nfor j in range(2):\n    k=np.argmax(np.abs(loadings[:,j]))\n    if loadings[k,j] < 0:\n        loadings[:,j] *= -1\n        scores[:,j] *= -1\n    anchors.append(features[k])\nprint('shape',X.shape,'rows',len(df),'BCE',bce.min(),bce.max(),'missing',int(X.isna().sum().sum()))\nprint('variance_explained',pca.explained_variance_ratio_.tolist(),'cumulative',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum())\nprint('anchors',anchors)\nprint('TARGET_LOADINGS')\nfor var in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\n    i=features.index(var)\n    print(var, 'PC1',loadings[i,0], 'PC2',loadings[i,1])\nprint('TARGET_SCORE DISTRIBUTIONS across century observations')\nout=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':bce,'PC1':scores[:,0],'PC2':scores[:,1]}).sort_values('BCE',ascending=False)\nfor axis in ['PC1','PC2']:\n    print(axis,'minmax',out[axis].min(),out[axis].max(),'mean',out[axis].mean(),'std',out[axis].std())\n# decade? concise decade-range scores tabulated, retaining row granularity\nprint('SCORES all BCE descending')\nprint(out.to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.4f}'))\n# Correlations as a useful representation of variable-axis association\nprint('TARGET_CORR_PC')\nfor var in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\n    i=features.index(var)\n    print(var, 'PC1',np.corrcoef(X.iloc[:,i],scores[:,0])[0,1], 'PC2',np.corrcoef(X.iloc[:,i],scores[:,1])[0,1])\nPY\npython /workspace/capital_pca.py",
  "timeout_seconds": 60
}

Cultural Capital consists of Diversity of Pottery form, Diversity of Pottery Decoration. 4. Economic Capital consists of Depot, Sickle, Axes & Celts, Monument Size. 5. Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7. include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

Nothing staged specifies a moving window, per-century PCA, scaling recipe, or component orientation. The 39 rows are annual-bin observations at BCE 4300, 4200, …, 500, so a separate PCA per century cannot be estimated from one row per slice.\\n\\n**Recommended operational interpretation:** fit one PCA across all 39 century observations, using the 12 capital indicators as variables (exclude BCE/group/color); report the three target variables' *loadings* on PC1 and PC2 as their stable variable characterization, and separately report each century's *observation scores* on PC1/PC2 to show their time distribution. This is more defensible than inventing a multi-century window without documentary evidence. The repeated rows are not independent temporal replicates, so frame PCA descriptively. This interpretation distinguishes loadings (how each capital indicator aligns with an axis) from scores (where each century falls on the axes); “PC1 values” is best read as loadings given the question names the symbolic capital components, but both are useful to include.\\n\\n**Scale/sign proposal:** parse decimal commas; use the supplied Z-valued indicators as-is, centered by PCA, with no additional per-variable scaling (thus a covariance PCA on already standardized indicators; verify that all columns are indeed intended Z values). For a reproducible orientation, after fitting orient each component so the loading of the highest-absolute-loading variable on that component is positive; list the anchor per PC.       "function": {
        "name": "execute_command",
        "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Run global PCA on parsed capital indicators and report scores\",\"command\":\"cat > /workspace/capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\n\\npath='data/capital.csv'\\ndf=pd.read_csv(path, dtype=str)\\nfeatures=[c for c in df.columns if c not in ('BCE','group','color')]\\n# CSV encodes decimals with commas. Parse all 12 indicator fields explicitly.\\nX=df[features].apply(lambda col: pd.to_numeric(col.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='raise'))\\nbce=pd.to_numeric(df['BCE'], errors='raise').astype(int)\\nassert X.shape == (39,12) and X.notna().all().all()\\nassert sorted(bce.unique()) == list(range(500,4301,100))\\n# Use supplied Z-scale as-is; sklearn PCA centers but does not rescale variables.\\npca=PCA(n_components=2)\\nscores=pca.fit_transform(X)\\nloadings=pca.components_.T\\n# Deterministic sign orientation: largest absolute loading is positive for each axis.\\nanchors=[]\\nfor j in range(2):\\n    k=np.argmax(np.abs(loadings[:,j]))\\n    if loadings[k,j] < 0:\\n        loadings[:,j] *= -1\\n        scores[:,j] *= -1\\n    anchors.append(features[k])\\nprint('shape',X.shape,'rows',len(df),'BCE',bce.min(),bce.max(),'missing',int(X.isna().sum().sum()))\\nprint('variance_explained',pca.explained_variance_ratio_.tolist(),'cumulative',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum())\\nprint('anchors',anchors)\\nprint('TARGET_LOADINGS')\\nfor var in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n    i=features.index(var)\\n    print(var, 'PC1',loadings[i,0], 'PC2',loadings[i,1])\\nprint('TARGET_SCORE DISTRIBUTIONS across century observations')\\nout=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':bce,'PC1':scores[:,0],'PC2':scores[:,1]}).sort_values('BCE',ascending=False)\\nfor axis in ['PC1','PC2']:\\n    print(axis,'minmax',out[axis].min(),out[axis].max(),'mean',out[axis].mean(),'std',out[axis].std())\\n# decade? concise decade-range scores tabulated, retaining row granularity\\nprint('SCORES all BCE descending')\\nprint(out.to_string(index=False,float_format=lambda x:f'{x:.4f}'))\\n# Correlations as a useful representation of variable-axis association\\nprint('TARGET_CORR_PC')\\nfor var in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n    i=features.index(var)\\n    print(var, 'PC1',np.corrcoef(X.iloc[:,i],scores[:,0])[0,1], 'PC2',np.corrcoef(X.iloc[:,i],scores[:,1])[0,1])\\nPY\\npython /workspace/capital_pca.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":60}"

Parse all 12 indicator fields explicitly.\\nX=df[features].apply(lambda col: pd.to_numeric(col.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False), errors='raise'))\\nbce=pd.to_numeric(df['BCE'], errors='raise').astype(int)\\nassert X.shape == (39,12) and X.notna().all().all()\\nassert sorted(bce.unique()) == list(range(500,4301,100))\\n# Use supplied Z-scale as-is; sklearn PCA centers but does not rescale variables.\\npca=PCA(n_components=2)\\nscores=pca.fit_transform(X)\\nloadings=pca.components_.T\\n# Deterministic sign orientation: largest absolute loading is positive for each axis.\\nanchors=[]\\nfor j in range(2):\\n    k=np.argmax(np.abs(loadings[:,j]))\\n    if loadings[k,j] < 0:\\n        loadings[:,j] *= -1\\n        scores[:,j] *= -1\\n    anchors.append(features[k])\\nprint('shape',X.shape,'rows',len(df),'BCE',bce.min(),bce.max(),'missing',int(X.isna().sum().sum()))\\nprint('variance_explained',pca.explained_variance_ratio_.tolist(),'cumulative',pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2].sum())\\nprint('anchors',anchors)\\nprint('TARGET_LOADINGS')\\nfor var in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n    i=features.index(var)\\n    print(var, 'PC1',loadings[i,0], 'PC2',loadings[i,1])\\nprint('TARGET_SCORE DISTRIBUTIONS across century observations')\\nout=pd.DataFrame({'BCE':bce,'PC1':scores[:,0],'PC2':scores[:,1]}).sort_values('BCE',ascending=False)\\nfor axis in ['PC1','PC2']:\\n    print(axis,'minmax',out[axis].min(),out[axis].max(),'mean',out[axis].mean(),'std',out[axis].std())\\n# decade? Symbolic capital consists of Hatchet & Swords, Daggers, House Size. 2. Social Capital consists of Copper and Gold, Amber, Monument Count 3. Cultural Capital consists of Diversity of Pottery form, Diversity of Pottery Decoration. 4. Economic Capital consists of Depot, Sickle, Axes & Celts, Monument Size. 5. Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates.
